22 May  - Adding species to the db and creating the db table.

I learnt about the UNSIGNED command in SQL which basically means that you cant store a negative number in a column but that
it uses the negative num storage for the positive to double the space.
However I did not know that previously so I have to alter the previous families table because foreign key must have the exact 
data type of what it is referencing.

to check data types in table: SHOW CREATE TABLE families\G

TO CHANGE THE DATATYPE:
ALTER TABLE families
MODIFY family_id INT UNSIGNED NOT NULL AUTO_INCREMENT;

THEN I GOT AN ERROR THAT WONT ALLOW AN AUTO INCREMENT COLUMN (PLANT_ID) TO BE USED FOR A GENERATED COLUMN (WAND_ID)
SO THE following WAS USED:

CREATE TABLE plants (
    plant_id INT UNSIGNED NOT NULL AUTO_INCREMENT,
    wand_id  VARCHAR(20) NOT NULL,
    genus    VARCHAR(255),
    species  VARCHAR(255),
    gbif_key VARCHAR(15),
    family_id INT UNSIGNED NOT NULL,
    PRIMARY KEY (plant_id),
    UNIQUE (wand_id),
    CONSTRAINT fk_plant_family
        FOREIGN KEY (family_id)
        REFERENCES families(family_id)
        ON UPDATE CASCADE
        ON DELETE RESTRICT
);

#########################################################dROPPED THIS TRIGGER

         DELIMITER $$
this misbehave and has been REMOVED
          CREATE TRIGGER trg_plants_wand_id
          AFTER INSERT ON plants
          FOR EACH ROW
          BEGIN
              UPDATE plants
              SET wand_id = CONCAT('P', NEW.plant_id)
              WHERE plant_id = NEW.plant_id;
          END$$

DELIMITER ;
THIS TRIGGER CAUSES TOO MANY PROBLEMS AND HAS BEEN REMOVED


LOAD DATA LOCAL INFILE '/localdata/wand_data/SANCDB_plants_for_DB.csv'
INTO TABLE plants
FIELDS TERMINATED BY ','
ENCLOSED BY '"'
LINES TERMINATED BY '\n'
IGNORE 1 LINES
(wand_id, genus, species, gbif_key, family_id);

It worked but the table was auto incrementing incorrectly so i cleared the table and it doest work now. kms
i FOUND THE ERROR IT WAS IN THE LAST LINE OF THE LOADFROM FILE BUT I FIXED IT AND NOW EVERYTHING LOOKS FINE.

CREATE TABLE diseases (
disease_id INT UNSIGNED NOT NULL AUTO_INCREMENT,
wand_id    VARCHAR(20),
code       VARCHAR(20)  NOT NULL,
title      VARCHAR(255) NOT NULL,
path       TEXT         NOT NULL,
PRIMARY KEY (disease_id),
UNIQUE (wand_id));

LOAD DATA LOCAL INFILE '/localdata/wand_data/diseases_with_materialized_paths.csv'
INTO TABLE diseases
FIELDS TERMINATED BY ','
ENCLOSED BY '"'
LINES TERMINATED BY '\n'
IGNORE 1 LINES
(code, title, path);

UPDATE diseases
SET wand_id = CONCAT('d', disease_id)
WHERE wand_id IS NULL OR wand_id = '';

As far as I can tell all is good and the diseases are all added to the DB.

27 May - Cleaning the NP classifier data and adding it to the DB

I so previously the NP classifier pathways superclasses and classes have been organized into db friendly fromat I have
now sent them to the /wand_data/ and will later add to the DB

only issue is according to the paper: "NPClassifier classifies the structure of an NP at three levels into seven Pathways, 
70 Superclasses, and 672 Classes" I have 7 pathways 75 superclasses and 651 classes. I am hoping when I start mapping 
compounds that this will be an easier thing to solve.

ADDING TO wandDB

CREATE TABLE npclassifier_pathway (
pathway_id INT UNSIGNED NOT NULL AUTO_INCREMENT,
pathway VARCHAR(40) NOT NULL,
PRIMARY KEY (pathway_id));

the below was used but had to be removed as the approach changed please see below
                                    CREATE TABLE npclassifier_superclass (
                                    superclass_id INT UNSIGNED NOT NULL AUTO_INCREMENT,
                                    superclass TEXT NOT NULL,
                                    pathway_id INT UNSIGNED NOT NULL,
                                    PRIMARY KEY (superclass_id),
                                    CONSTRAINT fk_superclass_pathway
                                    FOREIGN KEY (pathway_id)
                                    REFERENCES npclassifier_pathway(pathway_id)
                                    ON UPDATE CASCADE
                                    ON DELETE RESTRICT);
                                    
                                    CREATE TABLE npclassifier_class (
                                    class_id INT UNSIGNED NOT NULL AUTO_INCREMENT,
                                    class TEXT NOT NULL,
                                    superclass_id INT UNSIGNED NOT NULL,
                                    PRIMARY KEY (class_id),
                                    CONSTRAINT fk_class_superclass
                                    FOREIGN KEY (superclass_id)
                                    REFERENCES npclassifier_superclass(superclass_id)
                                    ON UPDATE CASCADE
                                    ON DELETE RESTRICT);

I created all the tables but  now I see there is an issue with the supeclass cs it has the class entries so imma fix that next
it looks like the pathway csv is correct tho

Oaky so something strange about the superclasses there are supposed to be 70 and I have 75 unique classes. When I deduplicate
on pathway and class there are 91 entries which makes it seem like the same class can belong to different pathways
Unique pathways: 7
Unique superclasses: 75
pathway
Shikimates and\nPhenylpropanoids    18
Polyketides                         16
Terpenoids                          16
Alkaloids                           15
Amino acids and Peptides            11
Fatty acids                         11
Carbohydrates                        4
= 91 

so it seems like this is normal and certain subclasses can belong to multiple pathways. In light of this it is Probably
best to change the layout of the DB. I think it would be best to drop the adjacency list idea and create junction tables
to assign these things to each other.

ADDING JUNCTION AND DB CSVS TO THE DB

CREATE TABLE npclassifier_superclass (
superclass_id INT UNSIGNED NOT NULL,
superclass TEXT NOT NULL,
PRIMARY KEY (superclass_id));

CREATE TABLE npclassifier_class (
class_id INT UNSIGNED NOT NULL,
class TEXT NOT NULL,
PRIMARY KEY (class_id));

CREATE TABLE junction_pathway_subclass (
pathway_id  INT UNSIGNED NOT NULL,
superclass_id INT UNSIGNED NOT NULL,
PRIMARY KEY (pathway_id, superclass_id),
FOREIGN KEY (pathway_id) REFERENCES npclassifier_pathway(pathway_id),
FOREIGN KEY (superclass_id) REFERENCES npclassifier_superclass(superclass_id));

CREATE TABLE junction_superclass_class (
superclass_id  INT UNSIGNED NOT NULL,
class_id INT UNSIGNED NOT NULL,
PRIMARY KEY (superclass_id, class_id),
FOREIGN KEY (superclass_id) REFERENCES npclassifier_superclass(superclass_id),
FOREIGN KEY (class_id) REFERENCES npclassifier_class(class_id));

LOAD DATA LOCAL INFILE '/localdata/wand_data/NpClassifierPathwaysDB.csv'
INTO TABLE npclassifier_pathway
FIELDS TERMINATED BY ','
ENCLOSED BY '"'
LINES TERMINATED BY '\n'
IGNORE 1 LINES
(pathway_id,pathway);

LOAD DATA LOCAL INFILE '/localdata/wand_data/NpClassifierSuperclassesDB.csv'
INTO TABLE npclassifier_superclass
FIELDS TERMINATED BY ','
ENCLOSED BY '"'
LINES TERMINATED BY '\n'
IGNORE 1 LINES
(superclass_id,superclass);

LOAD DATA LOCAL INFILE '/localdata/wand_data/NpClassifierClassesDB.csv'
INTO TABLE npclassifier_class
FIELDS TERMINATED BY ','
ENCLOSED BY '"'
LINES TERMINATED BY '\n'
IGNORE 1 LINES
(class_id,class);

LOAD DATA LOCAL INFILE '/localdata/wand_data/NpClassifierPathwaySuperclassJunctionDB.csv'
INTO TABLE junction_pathway_subclass
FIELDS TERMINATED BY ','
ENCLOSED BY '"'
LINES TERMINATED BY '\n'
IGNORE 1 LINES
(pathway_id,superclass_id);

LOAD DATA LOCAL INFILE '/localdata/wand_data/NpClassifierSuperclassClassJunctionDB.csv'
INTO TABLE junction_superclass_class
FIELDS TERMINATED BY ','
ENCLOSED BY '"'
LINES TERMINATED BY '\n'
IGNORE 1 LINES
(superclass_id,class_id);

looks like everything is added correctly

CREATE TABLE compounds (
compound_id  INT UNSIGNED NOT NULL AUTO_INCREMENT,
smiles VARCHAR(1000) NOT NULL,
inchi VARCHAR(2048) NOT NULL,
inchi_key VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
name VARCHAR(512),
synonyms TEXT,
coconut_id VARCHAR(20),
cas VARCHAR(200),
cid VARCHAR(200),
PRIMARY KEY (compound_id));


LOAD DATA LOCAL INFILE '/localdata/wand_data/anpdb_compounds_for_dbclean.csv'
INTO TABLE compounds
FIELDS TERMINATED BY ','
ENCLOSED BY '"'
LINES TERMINATED BY '\n'
IGNORE 1 LINES
(compound_id, smiles, inchi, inchi_key, name, synonyms, coconut_id, cas, cid);

24 June - finishing junction table for np classifier to compounds

So there is an issue. Sometimes the NPclassifier will leave the class entry blank. The Class, Superclass, Pathway junction table
heirarchy is therefore a bit fucked up because I was going to directly link the compound to a class with a junction table.

are also nan values for those 2 columns. 

The solutions I can think of:
1 - Store each compound with its full path in the compounds table. This is redundant and not normalised but it would mean 
I can probably get rid of the other junction tables and np classifier tables. 
2 - Junction table with the full path can be stored like above but I think that this is the normalized way. Actually I think
this is better than above but the only issue is that it feels redundant. There is already tables and junctions for the 
heirarchy between the pathway, superclass, class so it feels like I would just be rewriting that. Will the junction 
table of compounds and the npclassifier classification capture the heirarchy? I dont think it will do that effectively.
3 - There could be some sort of way of storing both the id of the classification and the level of the classification. I
just don't know how the relathionship will work out when creating the foreign key link in MYSQL.

I also manually added some of the compounds to the npclassifier web app and it looks like some of the goofier molecules
really cant be described. So I feel like option 2 is the best most database correct way to store it and I will build the
junction table accordingly with 4 foreign keys 1 for compound id and then an ID for each level of the classification.

looks like plants are on the I hope everything is good now to add to db: SQL:

           CREATE TABLE plants (
           plant_id  INT UNSIGNED NOT NULL,
           genus VARCHAR(50),
           species VARCHAR(50),
           gbif_key INT UNSIGNED,
           synonyms TEXT,
           family_id INT UNSIGNED NOT NULL,
           PRIMARY KEY (plant_id),
           FOREIGN KEY (family_id) REFERENCES families(family_id));
           
           LOAD DATA LOCAL INFILE '/localdata/wand_data/plants_to_send_to_db.csv'
           INTO TABLE plants
           FIELDS TERMINATED BY ','
           ENCLOSED BY '"'
           LINES TERMINATED BY '\n'
           IGNORE 1 LINES
           (plant_id,genus, species, gbif_key, synonyms, family_id);

Tomorrows problem

now to add the relationships to the db:

CREATE TABLE junction_compound_plant (
compound_id  INT UNSIGNED NOT NULL,
plant_id INT UNSIGNED NOT NULL,
PRIMARY KEY (compound_id, plant_id),
FOREIGN KEY (compound_id) REFERENCES compounds(compound_id),
FOREIGN KEY (plant_id) REFERENCES plants(plant_id));

LOAD DATA LOCAL INFILE '/localdata/wand_data/compound_plant_relationships.csv'
INTO TABLE junction_compound_plant
FIELDS TERMINATED BY ','
ENCLOSED BY '"'
LINES TERMINATED BY '\n'
IGNORE 1 LINES
(compound_id,plant_id);
